I know it sounds odd, but to me they’re real. Those ballads my brother kept aren’t just songs. I hear warnings in the words, like they’re tucked in there for me. And if I don’t get the memorial right, I do feel like something could actually happen. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, but I know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would see the memorial as a way to prevent something bad from happening, like you do?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think most people would see it that way. My wife doesn’t. She thinks I’m reading too much into those songs. But I’m certain those old ballads my brother kept have warnings in them for me, and if I get the memorial wrong… I just feel like I’d be ignoring what I heard.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, but I know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would see the memorial as a way to prevent something bad from happening, like you do?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think most people would see it that way. My wife doesn’t. She thinks I’m reading too much into those songs. But I’m certain those old ballads my brother kept have warnings in them for me, and if I get the memorial wrong… I just feel like I’d be ignoring what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Would you change your plans for the memorial if someone you trust could convince you that your interpretations of the old ballads are not accurate?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I would, honestly. If someone I trust talked to me, I’d listen, but… I’m still certain those ballads my brother kept aren’t just songs. There are warnings in the words, and they feel meant for me. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, but I know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these things for the memorial because you're trying to escape a feeling of anxiety or unease if you don't?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden, my walking group, pretty much everything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things for the memorial, or is it more that you want to make it perfect?\n\nPatient: It feels more like I have to keep doing it until it’s right. I start with one thing, like a song list, and then I’m calling people, making plans for displays, thinking about trips connected to it. Most days end up getting taken over by it, and my wife says I’ve let the garden and my walking group and everything else slide.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can't stop thinking about the memorial even when you're trying to do other things?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even when I’m trying to do something else, my mind keeps jumping around. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF42.2, F63.9, F32.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess what brings me in is this memorial I’m putting together for my brother. It started as something small, but it’s gotten a lot bigger than I meant it to. I’ve been spending most days chasing down songs, calling people, planning displays and even trips around it. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden, my walking group, pretty much everything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things for the memorial, or is it more that you want to make it perfect?\n\nPatient: It feels more like I have to keep doing it until it’s right. I start with one thing, like a song list, and then I’m calling people, making plans for displays, thinking about trips connected to it. Most days end up getting taken over by it, and my wife says I’ve let the garden and my walking group and everything else slide.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can't stop thinking about the memorial even when you're trying to do other things?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even when I’m trying to do something else, my mind keeps jumping around. One minute I’m thinking about a display case, then I’m thinking I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then some verse from an old ballad pops in. It’s hard to slow it down enough to put it all in order.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like something bad would happen if you didn't get the memorial exactly right?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know it sounds strange, but I feel like there are warnings in those old ballads my brother kept. They’re not just songs to me. Certain words feel like they’re meant for me, like if I miss them or get the memorial wrong, something could go wrong. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, but I know what I heard in those words.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would understand the warnings in those old ballads the same way you do?\n\nPatient: I don’t think everyone would hear it the same way, no. My wife sure doesn’t. She says they’re just old songs and I’m making too much of it. But to me, the words stand out like they’re meant for me, like there are warnings tucked in there. I know that sounds odd, but I’m certain of what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these warnings in the old ballads are real, something that could actually happen if you don't get the memorial right?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know it sounds odd, but to me they’re real. Those ballads my brother kept aren’t just songs. I hear warnings in the words, like they’re tucked in there for me. And if I don’t get the memorial right, I do feel like something could actually happen. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, but I know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would see the memorial as a way to prevent something bad from happening, like you do?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think most people would see it that way. My wife doesn’t. She thinks I’m reading too much into those songs. But I’m certain those old ballads my brother kept have warnings in them for me, and if I get the memorial wrong… I just feel like I’d be ignoring what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Would you change your plans for the memorial if someone you trust could convince you that your interpretations of the old ballads are not accurate?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I would, honestly. If someone I trust talked to me, I’d listen, but… I’m still certain those ballads my brother kept aren’t just songs. There are warnings in the words, and they feel meant for me. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, but I know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these things for the memorial because you're trying to escape a feeling of anxiety or unease if you don't?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I do feel uneasy if I think about not doing it right, yes. But it doesn’t feel like I’m just trying to calm myself down. It feels more like… I’m supposed to do it a certain way, and if I don’t, I’d be ignoring something important.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're performing these tasks for the memorial because you're being driven to do them, or because you genuinely believe they need to be done in a certain way?\n\nPatient: It’s more that I genuinely believe it has to be done a certain way. I know it sounds odd, but those old ballads my brother kept… they don’t feel like just songs to me. I’m certain there are warnings tucked in the words, meant for me. So when my wife says I’m reading too much into it, I hear her, but I still know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to change your mind about the warnings you hear in the old ballads?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I know how it sounds, and I know my wife thinks I’m reading too much into those songs. But to me, they’re not just old ballads my brother kept. There are warnings in the words, and they feel meant for me. I know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that could ever make you doubt the warnings you think you hear in the old ballads?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there is, no. I know it sounds strange, but those ballads my brother kept… they’re not just songs to me. I’m certain there are warnings in the words, meant for me. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, and I hear her, but I still know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings from the old ballads might be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about that, but… no, I don’t really think it’s mistaken. I know it sounds odd, and I know my wife thinks I’m reading too much into those old ballads, but I’m certain there are warnings in the words. They feel like they’re meant for me, and I know what I heard.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It started as something small, but it’s gotten a lot bigger than I meant it to. I’ve been spending most days chasing down songs, calling people, planning displays and even trips around it. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden, my walking group, pretty much everything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things for the memorial, or is it more that you want to make it perfect?\n\nPatient: It feels more like I have to keep doing it until it’s right. I start with one thing, like a song list, and then I’m calling people, making plans for displays, thinking about trips connected to it. Most days end up getting taken over by it, and my wife says I’ve let the garden and my walking group and everything else slide.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can't stop thinking about the memorial even when you're trying to do other things?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even when I’m trying to do something else, my mind keeps jumping around. One minute I’m thinking about a display case, then I’m thinking I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then some verse from an old ballad pops in. I know it sounds strange, but I feel like there are warnings in those old ballads my brother kept. They’re not just songs to me. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF42.2, F63.9, F32.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess what brings me in is this memorial I’m putting together for my brother. It started as something small, but it’s gotten a lot bigger than I meant it to. I’ve been spending most days chasing down songs, calling people, planning displays and even trips around it. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden, my walking group, pretty much everything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things for the memorial, or is it more that you want to make it perfect?\n\nPatient: It feels more like I have to keep doing it until it’s right. I start with one thing, like a song list, and then I’m calling people, making plans for displays, thinking about trips connected to it. Most days end up getting taken over by it, and my wife says I’ve let the garden and my walking group and everything else slide.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can't stop thinking about the memorial even when you're trying to do other things?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even when I’m trying to do something else, my mind keeps jumping around. One minute I’m thinking about a display case, then I’m thinking I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then some verse from an old ballad pops in. It’s hard to slow it down enough to put it all in order.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like something bad would happen if you didn't get the memorial exactly right?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know it sounds strange, but I feel like there are warnings in those old ballads my brother kept. They’re not just songs to me. Certain words feel like they’re meant for me, like if I miss them or get the memorial wrong, something could go wrong. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, but I know what I heard in those words.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would understand the warnings in those old ballads the same way you do?\n\nPatient: I don’t think everyone would hear it the same way, no. My wife sure doesn’t. She says they’re just old songs and I’m making too much of it. But to me, the words stand out like they’re meant for me, like there are warnings tucked in there. I know that sounds odd, but I’m certain of what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these warnings in the old ballads are real, something that could actually happen if you don't get the memorial right?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know it sounds odd, but to me they’re real. Those ballads my brother kept aren’t just songs. I hear warnings in the words, like they’re tucked in there for me. And if I don’t get the memorial right, I do feel like something could actually happen. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, but I know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would see the memorial as a way to prevent something bad from happening, like you do?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think most people would see it that way. My wife doesn’t. She thinks I’m reading too much into those songs. But I’m certain those old ballads my brother kept have warnings in them for me, and if I get the memorial wrong… I just feel like I’d be ignoring what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Would you change your plans for the memorial if someone you trust could convince you that your interpretations of the old ballads are not accurate?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I would, honestly. If someone I trust talked to me, I’d listen, but… I’m still certain those ballads my brother kept aren’t just songs. There are warnings in the words, and they feel meant for me. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, but I know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these things for the memorial because you're trying to escape a feeling of anxiety or unease if you don't?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I do feel uneasy if I think about not doing it right, yes. But it doesn’t feel like I’m just trying to calm myself down. It feels more like… I’m supposed to do it a certain way, and if I don’t, I’d be ignoring something important.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're performing these tasks for the memorial because you're being driven to do them, or because you genuinely believe they need to be done in a certain way?\n\nPatient: It’s more that I genuinely believe it has to be done a certain way. I know it sounds odd, but those old ballads my brother kept… they don’t feel like just songs to me. I’m certain there are warnings tucked in the words, meant for me. So when my wife says I’m reading too much into it, I hear her, but I still know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to change your mind about the warnings you hear in the old ballads?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I know how it sounds, and I know my wife thinks I’m reading too much into those songs. But to me, they’re not just old ballads my brother kept. There are warnings in the words, and they feel meant for me. I know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that could ever make you doubt the warnings you think you hear in the old ballads?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there is, no. I know it sounds strange, but those ballads my brother kept… they’re not just songs to me. I’m certain there are warnings in the words, meant for me. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, and I hear her, but I still know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings from the old ballads might be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about that, but… no, I don’t really think it’s mistaken. I know it sounds odd, and I know my wife thinks I’m reading too much into those old ballads, but I’m certain there are warnings in the words. They feel like they’re meant for me, and I know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings from the old ballads could ever change?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it would change, no. I know I’ve said it sounds odd, but those old ballads my brother kept don’t feel like just songs to me. There are warnings tucked into the words, and they’re for me. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, but I still know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the warnings you hear in the old ballads are just a product of your own mind?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. I know it sounds odd, and I understand why you’d ask, but those old ballads my brother kept… they aren’t just songs to me. I’m certain there are warnings tucked in the words for me. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, but I still know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these things for the memorial because you're trying to follow a specific set of rules or rituals that you feel you must follow?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it rules or rituals. I mean, I do feel like certain things should be done right for the memorial, but I don’t know that there’s a set pattern I’m following. I just… I’m trying to make sure it’s handled the way it should be.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I know that sounds odd, but I’m certain of what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these warnings in the old ballads are real, something that could actually happen if you don't get the memorial right?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know it sounds odd, but to me they’re real. Those ballads my brother kept aren’t just songs. I hear warnings in the words, like they’re tucked in there for me. And if I don’t get the memorial right, I do feel like something could actually happen. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, but I know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would see the memorial as a way to prevent something bad from happening, like you do?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think most people would see it that way. My wife doesn’t. She thinks I’m reading too much into those songs. But I’m certain those old ballads my brother kept have warnings in them for me, and if I get the memorial wrong… I just feel like I’d be ignoring what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Would you change your plans for the memorial if someone you trust could convince you that your interpretations of the old ballads are not accurate?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I would, honestly. If someone I trust talked to me, I’d listen, but… I’m still certain those ballads my brother kept aren’t just songs. There are warnings in the words, and they feel meant for me. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, but I know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these things for the memorial because you're trying to escape a feeling of anxiety or unease if you don't?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I do feel uneasy if I think about not doing it right, yes. But it doesn’t feel like I’m just trying to calm myself down. It feels more like… I’m supposed to do it a certain way, and if I don’t, I’d be ignoring something important.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're performing these tasks for the memorial because you're being driven to do them, or because you genuinely believe they need to be done in a certain way?\n\nPatient: It’s more that I genuinely believe it has to be done a certain way. I know it sounds odd, but those old ballads my brother kept… they don’t feel like just songs to me. I’m certain there are warnings tucked in the words, meant for me. So when my wife says I’m reading too much into it, I hear her, but I still know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to change your mind about the warnings you hear in the old ballads?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. There are warnings in the words, and they feel meant for me. I know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that could ever make you doubt the warnings you think you hear in the old ballads?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there is, no. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, but I know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these things for the memorial because you're trying to escape a feeling of anxiety or unease if you don't?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I do feel uneasy if I think about not doing it right, yes. But it doesn’t feel like I’m just trying to calm myself down. It feels more like… I’m supposed to do it a certain way, and if I don’t, I’d be ignoring something important.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're performing these tasks for the memorial because you're being driven to do them, or because you genuinely believe they need to be done in a certain way?\n\nPatient: It’s more that I genuinely believe it has to be done a certain way. I know it sounds odd, but those old ballads my brother kept… they don’t feel like just songs to me. I’m certain there are warnings tucked in the words, meant for me. So when my wife says I’m reading too much into it, I hear her, but I still know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to change your mind about the warnings you hear in the old ballads?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I know how it sounds, and I know my wife thinks I’m reading too much into those songs. But to me, they’re not just old ballads my brother kept. There are warnings in the words, and they feel meant for me. I know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that could ever make you doubt the warnings you think you hear in the old ballads?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there is, no. I know it sounds strange, but those ballads my brother kept… they’re not just songs to me. I’m certain there are warnings in the words, meant for me. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, and I hear her, but I still know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings from the old ballads might be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about that, but… no, I don’t really think it’s mistaken. I know it sounds odd, and I know my wife thinks I’m reading too much into those old ballads, but I’m certain there are warnings in the words. They feel like they’re meant for me, and I know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings from the old ballads could ever change?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it would change, no. I know I’ve said it sounds odd, but those old ballads my brother kept don’t feel like just songs to me. There are warnings tucked into the words, and they’re for me. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, but I still know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the warnings you hear in the old ballads are just a product of your own mind?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. I know it sounds odd, and I understand why you’d ask, but those old ballads my brother kept… they aren’t just songs to me. I’m certain there are warnings tucked in the words for me. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, but I still know what I heard.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these things for the memorial because you're trying to follow a specific set of rules or rituals that you feel you must follow?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it rules or rituals. I mean, I do feel like certain things should be done right for the memorial, but I don’t know that there’s a set pattern I’m following. I just… I’m trying to make sure it’s handled the way it should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being forced to think about the memorial and its details all the time, even when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say forced. I just keep coming back to it because it feels important.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these things for the memorial because you're trying to prevent something bad from happening, or because you feel compelled to do them in a specific way?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know.
